69	\paragraph{Beam width ablation.}
70	Table~\ref{tab:beam-ablation} varies beam width $k \in \{1,2,4,8\}$ on Qwen3-8B / SciERC (551 documents) under full S1 constraints.
71	
72	\begin{table}[h]
73	\centering
74	\small
75	\caption{Effect of beam width on constraint satisfaction and extraction quality (Qwen3-8B, SciERC, 551 documents, full S1 constraints). $k{=}1$ corresponds to greedy decoding.}
76	\label{tab:beam-ablation}
77	\begin{tabular}{@{}rcccc@{}}
78	\toprule
79	$k$ & DR$\,\downarrow$ & CF$\,\downarrow$ & E-F1$\,\uparrow$ & R-F1$\,\uparrow$ \\
80	\midrule
81	1 (greedy) & 0.93 & 9.90 & 30.98 & 8.36 \\
82	2          & 0.35 & 4.12 & 31.54 & \textbf{8.75} \\
83	4          & \textbf{0}    & 0.53 & 32.00 & 8.56 \\
84	8          & \textbf{0}    & \textbf{0.08} & \textbf{32.18} & 8.39 \\
85	\bottomrule
86	\end{tabular}
87	\end{table}
88	
89	DR elimination at $k{\geq}4$ confirms that constraint-search failure is resolved with moderate exploration width.
90	CF continues to decrease with larger beam (0.08\% at $k{=}8$, only 2 residual violations), while entity extraction quality improves monotonically.
91	$k{=}4$ represents the optimal cost-effectiveness trade-off: it eliminates all DR violations at roughly half the compute cost of $k{=}8$.
92	
93	\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}
94	Under greedy decoding, the fine-tuned Qwen3-8B (LoRA, SciERC) with full S1 constraints already achieves DR=0\% but retains CF=1.18\% (21 cross-field violations across 551 documents), with E-F1=67.05 and R-F1=43.77.
95	Adding beam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all residual CF violations (1.18\%\,$\to$\,0\%), yielding zero violations across all four metrics (ET=0\%, RV=0\%, DR=0\%, CF=0\%) while preserving extraction quality (E-F1=66.88, R-F1=44.23; changes within $\pm$0.5\,pp).
96	Llama-3.1-8B (LoRA, SciERC) with full S1 constraints and beam search ($k{=}4$) likewise achieves zero violations across all four metrics (ET=0\%, RV=0\%, DR=0\%, CF=0\%) with R-F1=46.11; the zero-violation result thus generalizes across calibrated model families.
97	% exp_id: q1_llama_ft_beam_scierc
98	The three constraint mechanisms (grammar masking, semantic domain-range filtering, and beam exploration) are thus fully composable: each addresses a distinct violation source without interfering with the others or degrading task performance.
99	
100	Beam search on DocRED (zero-shot, 50 documents) introduces a modest increase in parse failures (3/50, 6\%) compared to greedy (1.1\%), as longer beam-generated sequences occasionally produce malformed JSON in complex documents.
101	With DR=0 in 50 DocRED documents for both calibrated models, the Clopper-Pearson 95\% CI upper bound is 5.8\%; the full-scale LoRA evaluation below (998 documents, CI${\approx}$0.3\%) provides stronger statistical confirmation.
102	
103	Table~\ref{tab:beam-docred-lora} reports LoRA + beam-4 results on the full DocRED dev set (998 documents), extending the fine-tuning composability evaluation from SciERC to the higher-$\bar{\kappa}$ regime.
104	Parse failure rates under LoRA beam-4 range from 1.3\% to 3.5\%, lower than the zero-shot rate.
105

### Tool result

_source line 10947 · 2026-05-20T04:00:37.195Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013BMdEGS3UNVqdcnUYukmFr`</summary>

```text
150	
151	Beam search itself adds 32\% latency (4 beams); constraint overhead increases from 10\% (greedy) to 16.5\% (beam) because each beam independently maintains grammar and semantic state.
152	The total 1.54$\times$ overhead is acceptable for offline batch IE; \texttt{early\_stopping} limits additional compute to partial-sequence exploration.
153	
154	We also explored a type-consensus constraint that forces identical entity types across all relations within a document.
155	However, CF violations occur precisely where the consensus type conflicts with domain-range constraints, creating a fundamental CF$\leftrightarrow$DR trade-off.
156	With DR-aware enforcement (skipping consensus when it would violate domain-range), the layer becomes a no-op (100\% skip rate on 551 SciERC documents and 50 DocRED documents).
157	This confirms that beam search resolves the CF$\leftrightarrow$DR tension through a fundamentally different mechanism: token-level exploration of paths satisfying both constraints simultaneously.
158	
```

_source line 11014 · 2026-05-20T08:26:15.419Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Research Reviewer (Reports #100-#105 in this session segment) for project `schema_semantic_constrained_ie`, targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25 deadline (~5 days remaining). My role is to independently verify experimental conclusions, audit paper claims against registry/evaluation data, monitor GPU utilization, and report findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. This session covered:
   - Report #100: Full 4-GPU progress panorama + P0 eval consistency reminder
   - Report #101: D065 kill+restart verification (old 2048 processes still alive)
   - Report #102: Dual audit — E_F1 vs E_F1_excl_fail consistency + greedy data dependency mapping
   - Report #103: d066 DocRED beam sweep failure audit (startup recovery false positive)
   - Report #104: d066 SciERC beam sweep 8/8 completion + Pareto analysis
   - Report #105: d065 Llama greedy 4096 completion milestone
   - Continuous d065/d066/d067 progress monitoring across multiple server restarts

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OntoCoDec**: Two-layer constrained decoding (Grammar via XGrammar + Semantic via TypeConstraintProcessor) for LLM-based IE
   - **max_new_tokens 2048 vs 4096**: Root cause of eval consistency issue — greedy decoding without early_stopping truncates at 2048, producing artificially better DR (fewer triples = fewer violations) but lower E-F1
   - **E_F1 vs E_F1_excl_fail**: E_F1 includes parse-failure penalty (gold entities from failed docs in recall denominator); E_F1_excl_fail excludes failed docs entirely. Table 2 uses E_F1 consistently.
   - **Parse failure confound**: Qwen 7-9% PF vs Llama 2-3% PF causes differential E_F1 depression, making cross-model E_F1 trends appear inconsistent (Qwen drops, Llama rises under constraints)
   - **d066 Pareto analysis**: LoRA beam sweep k∈{1,2,4,8} — k=2 con is Pareto optimal. Key insight: LoRA+constrained synergy so strong that even k=1 con eliminates DR (zero-shot needs k≥4)
   - **d067 seed variance**: Greedy decoding is fully deterministic (byte-identical on same hardware), cross-hardware σ < 0.5pp, SNR > 12x
   - **κ̄ (constraint tightness)**: SciERC κ̄=0.333 (loose), DocRED κ̄=0.842 (tight)
   - **Startup recovery false positives**: Server restarts cause orphaned RUNNING experiments to be auto-marked as "failed" — not real failures

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/appendix_beam_search.tex`** (LOCAL)
     - Core file for beam search appendix. Contains tables: tab:beam-search (ZS 50-doc), tab:beam-docred-lora (LoRA 998-doc), tab:beam-ablation (ZS beam width), tab:beam-latency
     - Line 117: Qwen greedy con = `\textit{pending}` — needs d065 Qwen 4096 data
     - Line 121: Llama greedy con = `0 0 0 0.16 83.40 44.83 5.0` — WAS 2048 truncated data, NOW needs replacement with d065 4096 eval results
     - Lines 69-91: tab:beam-ablation still shows zero-shot data (not yet updated with d066 LoRA data)
     - d066 LoRA beam sweep table was supposed to be written by worker_writing_d066-latex-table but location unclear

   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/section5_scierc_draft.tex`** (LOCAL)
     - Lines 120-147: Table 2 (tab:composability) — LoRA composability data from w5 eval (4096), NOT affected by d065
     - Qwen DocRED: unc DR=0.76 E-F1=82.15 / S1 DR=0.01 E-F1=81.43
     - Llama DocRED: unc DR=0.49 E-F1=83.93 / S1 DR=0.20 E-F1=85.23
     - Line 143: Caption says "DR ≤ 0.20% across both datasets with R-F1 within ±0.6 pp"

   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/section5_runtime.tex`** (LOCAL)
     - Line 12: "beam S1 eliminates all four violation types on SciERC for both models and on DocRED for Llama"
     - Line 19: Fixed to "one of three configurations" (from Report #97 CRITICAL)

   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/section5_crossmodel_draft.tex`** (LOCAL)
     - Table 3 (tab:cross-model): Three-model zero-shot validation

   - **Registry entries verified** (registry.yaml):
     - w5_qwen_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval: E_F1=82.15, E_F1_excl_fail=85.72, PF=7.11%, max_new_tokens=4096
     - w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval: E_F1=81.43, E_F1_excl_fail=85.69, PF=8.52%, max_new_tokens=4096
     - w5_llama_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval: E_F1=83.93, PF=2.3%, max_new_tokens=4096
     - w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval: entity_f1=85.23, DR=0.20%, PF=2.51%, max_new_tokens=4096
     - d066_beam_sweep: status=ANALYZING, 8/8 configs complete with full metrics
     - d067_seed_variance: status=DONE, greedy deterministic, σ<0.5pp

   - **d065 eval results** (remote, westd-16980):
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d065_eval_llama_unc_4096.json`: ET=0, RV=0.15%, DR=0.49%, CF=0.08%, E-F1=84.77, R-F1=44.55, PF=2.3%
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d065_eval_llama_con_4096.json`: ET=0, RV=0, DR=0.00%, CF=0.26%, E-F1=84.71, R-F1=44.70, PF=2.61%

   - **d066 beam sweep data** (remote, westd-21461):
     - All 8 files at `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d066_beam_sweep/qwen_scierc_beam{1,2,4,8}_{unconstrained,full_s1}.jsonl` — all 551 lines

   - **d065 Qwen greedy 4096** (remote, westd-21461):
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d065_qwen_docred_greedy_unc_4096.jsonl`: 878/998
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d065_qwen_docred_greedy_con_4096.jsonl`: 700/998

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **D065 kill+restart delay (Report #101)**: ROADMAP said 2048 runs "废弃" but old processes still running. Flagged as WARNING → Worker executed kill+restart, verified new PIDs with --max_new_tokens 4096.
   - **Multiple server restarts**: Caused startup recovery to falsely mark d065_qwen_greedy_4096 and d066_docred_beam_sweep as "failed". Not real failures — Workers relaunched processes each time. Noted in Report #103.
   - **d066 DocRED beam sweep failure**: Startup recovery artifact (`failure_reason: 'startup recovery: RUNNING with no cron and no server/pid info'`). k=4 200-doc data still valid.
   - **d066 beam8 con E_F1 null in registry**: Initially registered with null values, later filled in: E-F1=66.36, R-F1=43.65, DR=0%, CF=0%.
   - **ssh_read_only shell metacharacter blocks**: `2>/dev/null` redirect was blocked. Switched to commands without redirects.

```text
Now I have the full picture:

**w5 Llama DocRED unconstrained eval:**
- E_F1: 83.93, E_P: 84.93, E_R: 82.96
- R_F1: 43.72, R_P: 38.59, R_R: 50.41
- DR: 0.49%, RV: 0.15%, ET: 0, CF: not recorded in metrics
- PF: 2.3%, entities_pred: 19106, relations_pred: 16171
- Checkpoint: `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/llama_lora_docred_merged`


- Ran with constrained extraction on the same model, getting slightly better entity scores at 85.23 F1 and relation F1 of 44.23, with constraint violations dropping to 0.2%.
- Also tested an unconstrained version with 4096 token limit, achieving entity F1 of 84.77 and relation F1 of 44.55, with parse failures at 2.3% and constraint violations at 0.49%.
- The constrained variant shows marginally lower entity precision but maintains similar relation performance while significantly reducing constraint violations.
- Comparing the unconstrained results across different runs, I'm seeing identical predictions for entities and relations, suggesting stable model behavior.
- But here's the puzzle: despite matching raw outputs, the F1 scores differ by roughly 0.8 percentage points across all metrics, which points to a discrepancy in how the evaluation script is computing scores rather than differences in the model's actual predictions.

For the constrained variant, though, the outputs genuinely diverge—fewer entities and relations predicted, more parse failures, and different constraint violations—suggesting either the runs used different hardware or there was a code change between the two versions. Both Llama runs are on the same hardware, but the Qwen constrained run is on a different machine, which could explain the divergence.

For unconstrained, the counts match perfectly but the F1 scores differ, which points to the same underlying outputs being evaluated with different scripts. Looking at the registry, d065 seems to have merged the Llama results into the Qwen experiment entry, so the Llama evaluation may have been run separately but I need to verify whether d065 actually reused the w5 output files or generated new ones. where? The eval files are on westd-16980 at `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/d065_eval_llama_unc_4096.json` and `d065_eval_llama_con_4096.json`.

The key question: did d065 re-run inference or just re-evaluate existing outputs?

For unconstrained:
- entities_pred matches exactly (19106)
- relations_pred matches exactly (16171)
- PF matches exactly (2.3%, 23 failures)
- DR: 0.49% both = 80 violations both (check: w5 shows RV=0.15%, d065 also RV=0.15%)

So unconstrained output is literally the same — same counts of everything. But F1 differs by +0.84pp. This MUST be a different eval script or different gold data/evaluation method.

For constrained, the numbers diverge significantly: d065 has 19 fewer predicted entities, 32 fewer relations, one additional parse failure, and zero detection rate violations compared to w5's 33. This suggests d065 actually re-ran the constrained inference, but the unconstrained results are identical, which is puzzling given the registry notes claim both were re-run. If greedy decoding is deterministic, maybe something else explains the identical unconstrained counts despite the F1 difference. The eval script used for d065 was run by a specific worker, and I need to figure out which one to understand the F1 discrepancy. For w5, the metrics likely came from the extraction script itself or a separate pipeline. The core issue is clear: the unconstrained outputs are identical but F1 scores differ by 0.84 points, which points entirely to eval script differences rather than model behavior. The constrained outputs diverged significantly with fewer entities and relations in d065, suggesting the constraint processor may have changed between versions.

For the report, I need to ensure Table 2 uses consistent eval methodology across all numbers, and I should verify whether the DR improvement from 0.20% to 0.00% represents genuine code changes or just different evaluation approaches. Let me check the server for evidence of code changes between these runs.

The F1 discrepancy must be coming from the evaluation script itself. Let me trace through how eval_d064.py computes F1, since w5 shows 83.93 while d065 shows 84.77, and they're using different evaluation methods.

The key difference is likely E_F1 versus E_F1_excl_fail—the latter excludes documents that failed constraint checking. Looking at the metrics, if d065's eval script is computing E_F1_excl_fail instead of the standard E_F1, that would account for the 0.84 percentage point increase. Both runs show the same 2.3% failure rate, so the gap narrows when excluding those failed documents. Looking at the actual numbers: with 23 failed docs out of 998 total, those missing gold entities reduce recall without contributing any true positives. The d065 model shows E_R=83.82 with E_P=85.75 giving F1=84.77, while w5 has E_R=82.96 with E_P=84.93 for F1=83.93—the difference in recall explains most of the 0.84pp gap between the two F1 scores.

But wait, precision is also different between them (84.93 vs 85.75), which shouldn't happen if both models are making the same 19106 predictions. That suggests either the TP counts differ or I'm misunderstanding how the evaluation handles failed documents. I should look at the actual evaluation script to understand what's happening.
```

import Path\\n\\nONTOLOGY_FILE = \\\"/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/data/docred/ontology_constraints_docred.json\\\"\\n\\nENTITY_TYPE_ALIASES = {\\n    'PERSON': 'PER', 'PEOPLE': 'PER',\\n raw.strip().upper()\\n    return ENTITY_TYPE_ALIASES.get(t, t)\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--input_path\\\", required=True)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--label\\\", default=\\\"\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output_json\\\", default=\\\"\\\")\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    with open(ONTOLOGY_FILE) as f:\\n        ontology = json.load(f)\\n\\n    VALID_ENTITY_TYPES = set(ontology[\\\"entity_types\\\"])\\n    RELATION_TYPES = ontology[\\\"relation_types\\\"]\\n    RELATION_CONSTRAINTS = ontology[\\\"relation_constraints\\\"]\\n\\n    name_to_pcode = {}\\n    for pcode, name in RELATION_TYPES.items():\\n        name_to_pcode[name.lower().strip()] = pcode\\n        name_to_pcode[name.strip()] = pcode\\n\\n    VALID_RELATION_NAMES = set(name.lower().strip() for name in RELATION_TYPES.values())\\n\\n    valid_pairs_by_pcode = {}\\n    for pcode, constraint in RELATION_CONSTRAINTS.items():\\n        valid_pairs_by_pcode[pcode] = set(tuple(p) for p in constraint[\\\"valid_pairs\\\"])\\n\\n    total_samples = 0\\n    parse_failures = 0\\n    total_entities_pred = 0\\n    total_relations_pred = 0\\n    et_violations = 0\\n    rv_violations = 0\\n    dr_violations = 0\\n    cf_violations = 0\\n\\n    entity_tp = entity_fp = entity_fn = 0\\n    relation_tp = relation_fp = relation_fn = 0\\n\\n    with open(args.input_path) as f:\\n        for line in f:\\n            total_samples += 1\\n            doc = json.loads(line)\\n            parsed = doc.get(\\\"parsed_output\\\")\\n            gold_entities = doc.get(\\\"gold_entities\\\", [])\\n            gold_relations = doc.get(\\\"gold_relations\\\", [])\\n\\n            if parsed is None or parsed == {}:\\n                parse_failures += 1\\n                entity_fn += len(set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in gold_entities))\\n                relation_fn += len(set((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], r[\\\"type\\\"]) for r in gold_relations))\\n                continue\\n\\n            pred_entities = parsed.get(\\\"entities\\\", [])\\n            pred_relations = parsed.get(\\\"relations\\\", [])\\n            total_entities_pred += len(pred_entities)\\n            total_relations_pred += len(pred_relations)\\n\\n            # ET violations\\n            for ent in pred_entities:\\n                if normalize_etype(ent.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\")) not in VALID_ENTITY_TYPES:\\n                    et_violations += 1\\n\\n            # Relation violations + CF\\n            entity_type_map = defaultdict(set)\\n            for rel in pred_relations:\\n                rel_name = rel.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\").lower().strip()\\n                head_type = rel.get(\\\"head_type\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n                tail_type = rel.get(\\\"tail_type\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n\\n                if rel.get(\\\"head\\\") and head_type:\\n                    entity_type_map[rel[\\\"head\\\"].lower()].add(normalize_etype(head_type))\\n                if rel.get(\\\"tail\\\") and tail_type:\\n                    entity_type_map[rel[\\\"tail\\\"].lower()].add(normalize_etype(tail_type))\\n\\n                if rel_name not in VALID_RELATION_NAMES:\\n                    rv_violations += 1\\n                    continue\\n\\n                pcode = name_to_pcode.get(rel_name)\\n                if pcode and pcode in valid_pairs_by_pcode:\\n                    pair = (normalize_etype(head_type), normalize_etype(tail_type))\\n                    if pair not in valid_pairs_by_pcode[pcode]:\\n                        dr_violations += 1\\n\\n            for entity_name, types in entity_type_map.items():\\n                if len(types) > 1:\\n                    cf_violations += 1\\n\\n            # Entity F1\\n            gold_ent_set = set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in gold_entities)\\n            pred_ent_set = set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in pred_entities)\\n            entity_tp += len(gold_ent_set & pred_ent_set)\\n            entity_fp += len(pred_ent_set - gold_ent_set)\\n            entity_fn += len(gold_ent_set - pred_ent_set)\\n\\n            # Relation F1\\n            gold_rel_set = set()\\n            for r in gold_relations:\\n                rtype_pcode = r[\\\"type\\\"]\\n                rtype_name = RELATION_TYPES.get(rtype_pcode, rtype_pcode).lower().strip()\\n                gold_rel_set.add((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], rtype_name))\\n\\n            pred_rel_set = set()\\n            for r in pred_relations:\\n                rtype_name = r.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\").lower().strip()\\n                pred_rel_set.add((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], rtype_name))\\n\\n            relation_tp += len(gold_rel_set & pred_rel_set)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

It was relaunched in its present form on 1st of April 2006 in the Athens metropolitan area , and gradually spread its coverage nationwide . Besides digital terrestrial transmission , it is available on the subscription - based encrypted services of Nova and Cosmote TV . Skai TV is also a member of Digea , a consortium of private television networks introducing digital terrestrial transmission in Greece . At launch , Skai TV opted for dubbing all foreign language content into Greek , instead of using subtitles .
